\medterm{Collagenase} An enzyme that breaks down collagen in connective tissue.

\synonyms
Collagen-digesting enzyme; collagen-degrading enzyme.

\medterm{Combined Hormone Therapy} Hormone therapy containing both estrogen and a progestogen, generally used when endometrial protection is needed in a person with a uterus.

\synonyms
Estrogen-progestogen therapy; combined menopausal hormone therapy.

\medterm{Combined Hyperlipidemia} A lipid disorder characterized by elevated cholesterol and triglycerides, often with increased atherogenic lipoproteins.

\synonyms
Mixed hyperlipidemia; combined hyperlipoproteinemia.

\medterm{Common Migraine} A migraine without a preceding aura.

\synonyms
Migraine without aura; migraine without neurologic aura.

\medterm{Compact Bone} Dense outer bone tissue organized into osteons that provides strength and structural support.

\synonyms
Cortical bone; dense bone.

\medterm{Complicated Migraine} An older, nonspecific term for migraine accompanied by prolonged or prominent neurologic symptoms; the specific syndrome should be identified when possible.

\synonyms
Migraine with prolonged neurologic symptoms; complex migraine, historical usage.

\medterm{Compounding Pharmacy} A pharmacy that prepares customized formulations for an individual patient when a suitable commercially manufactured product is unavailable or unsuitable.

\synonyms
Compounding pharmacy practice; extemporaneous pharmacy.

\medterm{Cones} Retinal photoreceptor cells responsible for color vision and detailed vision in relatively bright light.

\synonyms
Cone photoreceptors; cone cells.

\medterm{Conjugate Vaccine} A vaccine in which a poorly immunogenic polysaccharide antigen is linked to a carrier protein to improve the immune response, especially in infants and young children.

\synonyms
Protein-conjugate vaccine; conjugated polysaccharide vaccine.

\medterm{Conjugated Equine Estrogens} A mixture of estrogenic compounds historically derived from the urine of pregnant mares and used in some hormone-therapy formulations.

\synonyms
CEE; conjugated estrogens.

\medterm{Continuous Combined Hormone Therapy} A hormone regimen in which estrogen and progestogen are taken continuously rather than sequentially.

\synonyms
Continuous combined HRT; continuous estrogen-progestogen therapy.

\medterm{Contractile Proteins} Proteins, especially actin and myosin, that interact to generate force and shorten muscle cells.

\synonyms
Muscle contractile proteins; actomyosin proteins.

\medterm{Contrast Medium} A substance administered or placed in the body to make tissues, organs, or body spaces more visible during imaging.

\synonyms
Contrast agent; radiographic contrast material.

\medterm{Controllers} Long-term asthma medicines taken regularly to reduce airway inflammation and prevent symptoms or exacerbations.

\synonyms
Controller medicines; maintenance asthma therapy.

\medterm{Convulsion} A sudden episode of involuntary, often rhythmic muscle contractions that may occur with a seizure or other disturbance of brain function.

\synonyms
Convulsive episode; seizure-related motor activity.

\medterm{Corn} A localized thickening of skin caused by repeated pressure or friction, often occurring on the toes or feet.

\synonyms
Clavus; heloma.

\medterm{Coronary} Relating to the arteries or circulation that supply the heart muscle.

\synonyms
Cardiac blood-vessel-related; heart-coronary.

\medterm{Coronary Artery Bypass Surgery} Surgery that uses a graft to route blood around a narrowed or blocked coronary artery.

\synonyms
CABG; coronary bypass surgery.

\medterm{Coronary Endarterectomy} Surgical removal of atherosclerotic plaque from the inner lining of a coronary artery.

\synonyms
Coronary plaque endarterectomy; coronary endarterectomy procedure.

\medterm{Coronary Spasm} Temporary contraction of a coronary artery that reduces blood flow to the heart muscle and may cause angina or infarction.

\synonyms
Vasospastic angina mechanism; coronary artery spasm.

\medterm{Coronary Care Unit} A hospital unit providing continuous monitoring and specialized care for acute cardiac illness.

\synonyms
CCU; cardiac care unit.

\medterm{Corpus Cavernosum} One of two columns of erectile tissue in the penis that fill with blood during erection.

\synonyms
Cavernous body; penile corpus cavernosum.

\medterm{Corpus Luteum} The temporary endocrine structure formed from an ovarian follicle after ovulation; it produces progesterone during the luteal phase and early pregnancy.

\synonyms
Yellow body; luteal body.

\medterm{Corpus Spongiosum} A column of erectile tissue surrounding the penile urethra and expanding distally to form the glans.

\synonyms
Spongy body; corpus spongiosum penis.

\medterm{Corrugator Muscle} A small facial muscle that draws the eyebrows downward and inward, contributing to frowning.

\synonyms
Corrugator supercilii; glabellar muscle.

\medterm{Cortex} The outer layer of an organ or structure, such as the cerebral cortex, adrenal cortex, or outer layer of a hair shaft.

\synonyms
Cortical layer; outer cortex.

\medterm{Cortical Bone} Dense bone forming the outer shell of most bones and providing much of the skeleton's mechanical strength.

\synonyms
Compact bone; cortical tissue.

\medterm{Corticotropin-Releasing Factor} A hypothalamic hormone that stimulates the pituitary gland to release adrenocorticotropic hormone and helps coordinate the stress response.

\synonyms
Corticotropin-releasing hormone; CRH.

\medterm{COX-2 Inhibitors} Medicines that inhibit cyclooxygenase-2, an enzyme involved in prostaglandin production and inflammation.

\synonyms
Cyclooxygenase-2 inhibitors; selective COX-2 inhibitors.

\medterm{CPR} CPR is a procedure, device, treatment, or care intervention; indications, benefits, risks, and follow-up depend on the patient and clinical goal.

\medterm{Craving} A strong, often difficult-to-control desire for a substance, behavior, or experience, commonly seen in substance-use disorders.

\synonyms
Strong urge; compulsive desire.

\medterm{Creatinine Test} A blood or urine test measuring creatinine to help assess kidney filtration and muscle-related creatinine production.

\synonyms
Creatinine measurement; renal function creatinine test.

\medterm{Crepitus} A crackling, grating, or popping sensation or sound caused by movement of tissue, bone, joint surfaces, or air in soft tissue.

\synonyms
Crepitation; crackling sensation.

\medterm{Crohn's Disease} A chronic inflammatory bowel disease that can affect any part of the gastrointestinal tract and commonly causes abdominal pain, diarrhea, weight loss, or systemic inflammation.

\synonyms
Crohn disease; regional enteritis.

\medterm{Crown} The visible part of a tooth above the gum line, or a dental restoration that covers and protects a damaged tooth.

\synonyms
Dental crown; anatomic tooth crown.

\medterm{CT} Computed tomography, an imaging method that uses multiple X-ray measurements and computer reconstruction to produce cross-sectional images.

\synonyms
Computed tomography; CAT scan.

\medterm{Cupping} An abnormal enlargement or depression of the optic-disc cup, often assessed in evaluating glaucoma.

\synonyms
Optic-disc cupping; glaucomatous cupping.

\medterm{Cushing's Syndrome} A disorder caused by prolonged exposure to excessive cortisol, producing features such as central weight gain, hypertension, skin changes, and muscle weakness.

\synonyms
Hypercortisolism; cortisol excess syndrome.

\medterm{Cuticle} The thin outermost layer of a hair shaft formed by overlapping cells that protect the inner cortex.

\synonyms
Hair cuticle; cuticular layer.

\medterm{Cyclic Guanosine Monophosphate} A cellular signaling molecule involved in smooth-muscle relaxation, phototransduction, and other physiologic processes.

\synonyms
cGMP; cyclic GMP.

\medterm{Cyclic Hormone Therapy} Hormone therapy in which estrogen is given continuously or for part of a cycle and progestogen is added for a defined number of days.

\synonyms
Sequential hormone therapy; cyclic estrogen-progestogen therapy.

\medterm{Cyclooxygenase} An enzyme family that helps convert arachidonic acid into prostaglandins and related mediators involved in pain, inflammation, and platelet function.

\synonyms
COX enzyme; prostaglandin-endoperoxide synthase.

\medterm{Cytokines} Small signaling proteins released by immune and other cells that regulate inflammation, immunity, blood-cell production, and tissue responses.

\synonyms
Immune signaling proteins; cytokine mediators.

\medterm{Cytotoxic Alopecia} Hair loss caused by exposure to a cytotoxic treatment or other toxic injury to actively growing hair follicles.

\synonyms
Drug-induced alopecia; chemotherapy-related hair loss.

\medterm{C fiber} C fiber concerns c fiber, a biologic or physiologic concept used in research, laboratory medicine, and the explanation of disease.

\medterm{Cabazitaxel} Cabazitaxel is a medical term describing the named concept; its precise interpretation depends on its defining features, associated findings, and clinical context.

\medterm{Cabergoline} Cabergoline is a medical term describing the named concept; its precise interpretation depends on its defining features, associated findings, and clinical context.

\medterm{Cadherin} A calcium-dependent cell-adhesion protein that links neighboring cells and helps organize tissues; abnormal cadherin expression can promote invasion and metastasis in cancer.

\medterm{Cadmium} A toxic heavy metal found in tobacco smoke, batteries, pigments, and contaminated food; chronic exposure can injure the kidneys, lungs, and bones and increases cancer risk.

\medterm{Cadmium chloride} Cadmium chloride is a medical term describing the named concept; its precise interpretation depends on its defining features, associated findings, and clinical context.

\medterm{Cadusafos} An organophosphate pesticide that inhibits acetylcholinesterase; exposure can cause cholinergic poisoning with sweating, salivation, vomiting, weakness, breathing difficulty, or seizures.

\medterm{Caenorhabditis elegans} Caenorhabditis elegans identifies caenorhabditis elegans, a biological taxon or infectious-disease concept whose clinical importance depends on the organism, host, and site involved.

\medterm{Cafe-au-lait spot} A flat, evenly pigmented light-brown skin macule; several lesions or characteristic size and distribution may suggest neurofibromatosis type 1 or another genetic disorder.

\medterm{Caffeic acid} A plant-derived phenolic compound found in coffee, fruit, and vegetables; it participates in plant metabolism and is studied experimentally for antioxidant and other biologic effects.

\medterm{Calbindin} A calcium-binding protein that buffers intracellular calcium and supports signaling, especially in neurons and intestinal epithelial cells; it is also used as a tissue marker.

\medterm{Calcifediol} 25-hydroxyvitamin D, the major circulating storage form of vitamin D measured to assess vitamin-D status before activation to calcitriol.

\medterm{Calcineurin} A calcium- and calmodulin-dependent phosphatase that activates NFAT transcription factors; calcineurin inhibitors such as cyclosporine suppress T-cell activation.

\medterm{Calcitriol} The hormonally active form of vitamin D, produced by renal and other tissues, that increases intestinal calcium and phosphate absorption and regulates parathyroid hormone and bone metabolism.

\medterm{Calcium carbonate} Calcium carbonate concerns calcium carbonate, a biologic or physiologic concept used in research, laboratory medicine, and the explanation of disease.

\medterm{Calcium channel} Calcium channel concerns calcium channel, a biologic or physiologic concept used in research, laboratory medicine, and the explanation of disease.

\medterm{Calcium chloride} Calcium chloride is a medical term describing the named concept; its precise interpretation depends on its defining features, associated findings, and clinical context.

\medterm{Calcium citrate} Calcium citrate concerns calcium citrate, a biologic or physiologic concept used in research, laboratory medicine, and the explanation of disease.

\medterm{Calcium gluconate} Calcium gluconate concerns calcium gluconate, a biologic or physiologic concept used in research, laboratory medicine, and the explanation of disease.

\medterm{Calcium signaling} Calcium signaling concerns calcium signaling, a biologic or physiologic concept used in research, laboratory medicine, and the explanation of disease.

\medterm{Calculi} Hardened mineral concretions, such as gallstones, kidney stones, or salivary stones, formed when dissolved substances precipitate and aggregate within a body organ or duct.

\medterm{Calibration} Calibration is used in relation to calibration during assessment, diagnosis, treatment, monitoring, or patient support.

\medterm{Caliciviridae} Caliciviridae identifies caliciviridae, a biological taxon or infectious-disease concept whose clinical importance depends on the organism, host, and site involved.

\medterm{Calmodulin} An intracellular calcium-binding messenger protein that changes shape when it binds calcium and regulates enzymes, ion channels, muscle contraction, and gene expression.

\medterm{Calnexin} An endoplasmic-reticulum chaperone that binds newly synthesized glycoproteins and helps them fold correctly before they are transported or degraded.

\medterm{Calories} Units used to express the energy supplied by food or expended by the body; food labels generally report kilocalories, commonly called calories.

\medterm{Calorimetry} Calorimetry is used in relation to calorimetry during assessment, diagnosis, treatment, monitoring, or patient support.

\medterm{Calpain} Calpain is used in relation to calpain during assessment, diagnosis, treatment, monitoring, or patient support.

\medterm{Calponin} An actin-associated protein in smooth muscle and other cells that modulates contraction and cytoskeletal organization; immunostaining can help identify myoid differentiation.

\medterm{Calreticulin} An endoplasmic-reticulum chaperone and calcium-binding protein involved in protein folding and immune signaling; CALR mutations are associated with some myeloproliferative neoplasms.

\medterm{Calretinin} A calcium-binding protein expressed in selected neurons, mesothelial cells, and other tissues; immunohistochemical staining helps distinguish mesothelioma from some carcinomas.

\medterm{Calvarium} The dome-like portion of the skull formed mainly by the frontal, parietal, and occipital bones that encloses and protects the cerebral hemispheres.

\medterm{Campesterol} A plant sterol structurally related to cholesterol that is absorbed in small amounts from food; elevated absorption can contribute to rare sitosterolemia.

\medterm{Camphor} A fragrant bicyclic compound used in topical products and some traditional remedies; ingestion can cause nausea, agitation, seizures, and potentially fatal toxicity.

\medterm{Campomelic dysplasia} A disorder, malformation, or clinical finding involving the body structure or process named by the term; diagnosis uses its characteristic pattern, history, examination, and appropriate testing.

\medterm{Camptothecin} A plant-derived compound that inhibits topoisomerase I by stabilizing the enzyme--DNA cleavage complex; related drugs include irinotecan and topotecan.

\medterm{Camptothecin analogue} A drug structurally related to camptothecin that inhibits topoisomerase I, disrupting DNA replication and causing cancer-cell death; toxicity commonly includes marrow or gastrointestinal effects.

\medterm{Cannabinoid} A compound that acts on cannabinoid receptors or related targets, including plant cannabinoids, endogenous endocannabinoids, and synthetic agents; effects vary from analgesia and appetite change to intoxication.

\medterm{Cannabinoid receptor} A G-protein-coupled receptor, chiefly CB1 or CB2, activated by endocannabinoids and some cannabis compounds to modulate pain, appetite, mood, memory, immunity, and inflammation.

\medterm{Cannabinol} A mildly psychoactive cannabinoid produced as THC oxidizes; its clinical effects and therapeutic usefulness remain less established than those of several other cannabinoids.

\medterm{Carbamate} A chemical group found in pesticides and medicines; carbamate insecticides reversibly inhibit acetylcholinesterase and can cause a cholinergic toxidrome.

\medterm{Carbaryl} A carbamate insecticide that reversibly inhibits acetylcholinesterase; substantial exposure can cause sweating, salivation, vomiting, muscle weakness, and breathing problems.

\medterm{Carbazilquinone} An investigational quinone-containing anticancer compound studied for cytotoxic effects and bioreductive activity; it is not a routine clinical medicine.

\medterm{Carbenicillin} Carbenicillin is a medication or pharmacologic agent; its indication, dose, interactions, contraindications, and adverse effects depend on the specific product and patient.

\medterm{Carbofuran} A highly toxic carbamate pesticide that inhibits acetylcholinesterase and can cause rapid cholinergic poisoning after ingestion, inhalation, or skin exposure.

\medterm{Carbogen} A breathing mixture containing oxygen and carbon dioxide, used experimentally or in selected treatments to stimulate ventilation or modify tissue oxygenation.

\medterm{Carboprost} A prostaglandin F2-alpha analogue that contracts uterine muscle and is used for selected cases of postpartum hemorrhage or pregnancy termination; asthma is an important precaution.

\medterm{Carbosulfan} A carbamate insecticide that inhibits acetylcholinesterase and can cause cholinergic poisoning after significant exposure.

\medterm{Celosomy} A medical term describing the named biologic or clinical concept; its defining feature is the process, structure, exposure, or finding specified by the term, interpreted in context.

\medterm{Cementoma} A medical term describing the named biologic or clinical concept; its defining feature is the process, structure, exposure, or finding specified by the term, interpreted in context.

\medterm{Centigram} A metric unit of mass equal to one hundredth of a gram, or 10 milligrams, used for small quantities of medicines or specimens.

\medterm{Centimeter} Centimeter is used in relation to centimeter during assessment, diagnosis, treatment, monitoring, or patient support.

\medterm{Centipoise} A unit of dynamic viscosity equal to one millipascal-second, used to describe the flow resistance of blood, fluids, medicines, and other materials.

\medterm{Central apnea} A pause in breathing caused by absent or reduced respiratory effort from impaired brainstem signaling, rather than upper-airway obstruction.

\medterm{Centrifugation} Spinning a specimen at high speed to separate components by density, such as cells from plasma or precipitated material from a liquid.

\medterm{Centriole} A cylindrical microtubule structure in the centrosome that helps organize the mitotic spindle and, in some cells, forms the basal body of a cilium or flagellum.

\medterm{Centromere} The constricted chromosome region where sister chromatids remain attached and the kinetochore forms for accurate chromosome segregation during cell division.

\medterm{Centrosome} The main microtubule-organizing center of an animal cell, containing a pair of centrioles and coordinating the spindle during mitosis.

\medterm{Cephacetrile} An older first-generation cephalosporin antibiotic that inhibits bacterial cell-wall synthesis; it is not commonly used in current practice.

\medterm{Cephalexin} An oral first-generation cephalosporin that inhibits bacterial cell-wall synthesis and treats selected skin, respiratory, urinary, and other susceptible infections.

\medterm{Cephalometry} Measurement of the skull and facial structures, usually on standardized radiographs, used in orthodontic planning and assessment of craniofacial growth.

\medterm{Cephaloridine} An older injectable first-generation cephalosporin antibiotic whose use declined because of nephrotoxicity and the availability of safer alternatives.

\medterm{Cephalothin} An older injectable first-generation cephalosporin that inhibits bacterial cell-wall synthesis and was used for susceptible serious infections.

\medterm{Cephapirin} A first-generation cephalosporin antibiotic formerly used for susceptible bacterial infections; current availability and indications vary by country.

\medterm{Cephradine} A first-generation cephalosporin antibiotic related to cephalexin that inhibits bacterial cell-wall synthesis and treats selected susceptible infections.

\medterm{Cerebellar cortex} The folded outer layer of the cerebellum containing Purkinje and granular neurons that coordinates balance, posture, timing, and precision of movement.

\medterm{Cerebellar disease} A disorder of the cerebellum causing impaired coordination, gait ataxia, dysmetria, tremor, abnormal eye movements, or speech changes.

\medterm{Cerebellar dysmetria} Inability to control the range and force of a voluntary movement accurately, producing overshooting or undershooting from cerebellar dysfunction.

\medterm{Cerebellar hemangioblastoma} Cerebellar hemangioblastoma describes a neoplastic process involving the tissue or organ named by the term. Diagnosis establishes the tumor type, extent, biologic behavior, and appropriate management.

\medterm{Cerebellar hypoplasia} Underdevelopment of the cerebellum, usually beginning before birth, that may cause poor coordination, abnormal eye movements, seizures, and developmental impairment.

\medterm{Cerebellar neoplasm} Cerebellar neoplasm describes a neoplastic process involving the tissue or organ named by the term. Diagnosis establishes the tumor type, extent, biologic behavior, and appropriate management.

\medterm{Cerebellar nuclei} Deep clusters of cerebellar neurons that receive processed signals from the cerebellar cortex and send coordinated motor output to the brainstem and thalamus.

\medterm{Cerebellar vermis} The midline portion of the cerebellum that helps control trunk posture, gait, balance, and coordination of eye and head movements.

\medterm{Cerebral aqueduct} Cerebral aqueduct refers to cerebral aqueduct, an anatomical or physiologic concept used to describe a body structure, process, or function.

\medterm{Cerebral artery} Cerebral artery denotes cerebral artery, an anatomical structure or tissue whose clinical importance depends on location, connections, blood supply, innervation, and function.

\medterm{Cerebral atrophy} Cerebral atrophy refers to cerebral atrophy, an anatomical or physiologic concept used to describe a body structure, process, or function.

\medterm{Cerebral calcification} Cerebral calcification refers to cerebral calcification, an anatomical or physiologic concept used to describe a body structure, process, or function.

\medterm{Cerebral crus} Cerebral crus refers to cerebral crus, an anatomical or physiologic concept used to describe a body structure, process, or function.

\medterm{Cerebral hemisphere} Cerebral hemisphere refers to cerebral hemisphere, an anatomical or physiologic concept used to describe a body structure, process, or function.

\medterm{Cerebral ischemia} Cerebral ischemia refers to cerebral ischemia, an anatomical or physiologic concept used to describe a body structure, process, or function.

\medterm{Cerebral malaria} Cerebral malaria refers to cerebral malaria, an anatomical or physiologic concept used to describe a body structure, process, or function.

\medterm{Cerebral ventricle} Cerebral ventricle refers to cerebral ventricle, an anatomical or physiologic concept used to describe a body structure, process, or function.

\medterm{Cerebral ventriculography} Cerebral ventriculography refers to cerebral ventriculography, an anatomical or physiologic concept used to describe a body structure, process, or function.

\medterm{Cerebral ventriculomegaly} Cerebral ventriculomegaly refers to cerebral ventriculomegaly, an anatomical or physiologic concept used to describe a body structure, process, or function.

\medterm{Cerebrosides} Glycosphingolipids composed of ceramide plus a single sugar, abundant in myelin and nervous tissue and involved in membrane structure.

\medterm{Cerebrovascular disorder} A disorder of brain blood vessels or cerebral blood flow, including ischemia, hemorrhage, aneurysm, malformation, and venous thrombosis.

\medterm{Cerium} A rare-earth metal whose compounds have industrial and experimental medical uses; exposure to some forms can irritate tissues or cause toxicity.

\medterm{Certification} A formal process confirming that a person, service, laboratory, or facility meets specified training, quality, safety, or professional standards.

\medterm{Certolizumab pegol} Certolizumab pegol is a medication or pharmacologic agent; its indication, dose, interactions, contraindications, and adverse effects depend on the specific product and patient.

\medterm{Ceruminous adenocarcinoma} Ceruminous adenocarcinoma describes a neoplastic process involving the tissue or organ named by the term. Diagnosis establishes the tumor type, extent, biologic behavior, and appropriate management.

\medterm{Cervical} Relating either to the neck portion of the spine or to the cervix of the uterus; the intended meaning is determined by the surrounding anatomic context.

\medterm{Cervical arthritis} Degenerative or inflammatory disease of the neck joints that can cause neck pain, stiffness, reduced motion, and sometimes nerve or spinal-cord compression.

\medterm{Cervical atlas} The first cervical vertebra, C1, a ring-shaped bone that supports the skull and rotates around the second cervical vertebra.

\medterm{Cervical change} A change in the uterine cervix or cervical spine, depending on context; clinical significance is determined by the specific structural, inflammatory, or pregnancy-related finding.

\medterm{Cervical dislocation} Abnormal displacement of one cervical vertebra relative to another, usually from trauma, that can destabilize the neck and injure the spinal cord.

\medterm{Cervical endometriosis} Endometrial-like tissue in or on the cervix that may cause bleeding after sex, pelvic pain, or an abnormal cervical lesion.

\medterm{Cervical ganglion} A cluster of nerve-cell bodies in the sympathetic chain of the neck that relays autonomic signals to the head, neck, and upper chest.

\medterm{Cervical insufficiency} Painless shortening or opening of the cervix during pregnancy before term, increasing risk of second-trimester loss or spontaneous preterm birth.

\medterm{Cervical lymphadenopathy} Enlarged lymph nodes in the neck, commonly caused by infection but sometimes reflecting malignancy, autoimmune disease, or other systemic illness.

\medterm{Cervix carcinoma} Cervix carcinoma describes a neoplastic process involving the tissue or organ named by the term. Diagnosis establishes the tumor type, extent, biologic behavior, and appropriate management.

\medterm{Cervix disease} A disorder of the lower uterine cervix, ranging from infection and benign polyps to precancer, cancer, scarring, or pregnancy-related change.

\medterm{Cervix neoplasm} Cervix neoplasm describes a neoplastic process involving the tissue or organ named by the term. Diagnosis establishes the tumor type, extent, biologic behavior, and appropriate management.

\medterm{Cesium} A chemical element whose radioactive isotope cesium-137 can cause serious external or internal radiation injury after contamination or exposure.

\medterm{Cestoda} The tapeworm class of flatworms; human infection follows ingestion of eggs or larval tissue and can cause intestinal disease or tissue cysts.

\medterm{Cetirizine} A second-generation H1 antihistamine used for allergic rhinitis and urticaria; it is less sedating than older antihistamines but can still cause drowsiness.

\medterm{Cetoleic acid} A long-chain monounsaturated fatty acid found mainly in marine oils and studied for effects on lipid metabolism and cellular membranes.

\medterm{Cetrorelix} A gonadotropin-releasing hormone antagonist used in controlled ovarian stimulation to prevent a premature luteinizing-hormone surge.

\medterm{Cetuximab} Cetuximab is a medication or pharmacologic agent; its indication, dose, interactions, contraindications, and adverse effects depend on the specific product and patient.

\medterm{Cevimeline} A muscarinic receptor agonist used to stimulate salivary secretion and relieve dry mouth, especially in selected patients with Sjögren syndrome.

\medterm{Cevimeline hydrochloride} Cevimeline hydrochloride is a medicine or pharmacologic agent; its indication, dosing, contraindications, interactions, and adverse effects depend on the specific product and patient.

\medterm{Chaetomium} Chaetomium is a biological organism or infectious agent; its clinical importance depends on the species, host, site, exposure, and evidence of infection or colonization.

\medterm{Chagas cardiomyopathy} Chronic heart disease caused by Trypanosoma cruzi infection, with conduction defects, arrhythmias, ventricular dilation, heart failure, or thromboembolism.

\medterm{Challenge agent} A substance, organism, allergen, or physiologic stimulus deliberately administered in a controlled test to provoke and measure a response.

\medterm{Chamomile extract} An extract of chamomile flowers used in traditional and complementary products; allergic reactions and medicine interactions are possible, while evidence varies by indication.

\medterm{Chaplain} A trained spiritual-care professional who supports patients and families with religious, existential, emotional, or ethical concerns in healthcare settings.

\medterm{Cheek} The facial region forming the side wall of the mouth, containing skin, muscle, fat, salivary structures, nerves, and blood vessels.

\medterm{Cheek pouch} A distensible pocket inside the cheek found in some rodents and other animals, used to temporarily store and transport food.

\medterm{Cheilodynia} Pain of the lips, which may result from inflammation, infection, trauma, allergy, dryness, nerve pain, or another oral or dermatologic disorder.

\medterm{Cheilognathopalatoschisis} A congenital cleft involving the lip, jaw, and palate, caused by incomplete fusion of facial processes during embryonic development.

\medterm{Cheilognathoschisis} A congenital cleft of the lip and alveolar part of the upper jaw, with or without associated palatal involvement.

\medterm{Cheiromegaly} Abnormal enlargement of one or both hands, classically associated with excess growth hormone but also possible with local overgrowth or other disorders.

\medterm{Chelating agent} A compound that binds a metal ion through multiple chemical sites, allowing the metal to be measured, mobilized, or removed in selected toxic exposures.

\medterm{Chemexfoliation} Application of a chemical solution to remove controlled layers of skin, improving selected scars, pigmentation, wrinkles, or superficial lesions.

\medterm{Chemical} A substance defined by its molecular composition and properties; medical effects depend on dose, route, exposure duration, metabolism, and biological target.

\medterm{Chemical ablation} Destruction of unwanted tissue by injecting or applying a chemical irritant or sclerosant, used in selected lesions, vessels, cysts, or other conditions.

\medterm{Chemical burn} Tissue injury caused by a corrosive acid, alkali, solvent, or other chemical, which may continue to penetrate until the substance is removed and neutralized appropriately.

\medterm{Chemical carcinogenesis} Development of cancer after exposure to chemicals that cause DNA damage, abnormal cell signaling, chronic tissue injury, or altered gene regulation.

\medterm{Chemical cleavage} Breaking a chemical bond with a reagent, used in laboratory analysis, molecular biology, synthesis, or controlled degradation of a larger molecule.

\medterm{Chemical cofactor} A nonprotein chemical helper required for an enzyme or biologic reaction, such as a metal ion, vitamin-derived coenzyme, or other small molecule.

\medterm{Chemical fractionation} Separation of a mixture into fractions according to chemical or physical properties such as solubility, charge, size, or binding affinity.

\medterm{Chemical impurity} An unintended chemical present in a medicine, specimen, or material, potentially affecting safety, stability, measurement accuracy, or biologic activity.

\medterm{Chemistry} The study of substances, their composition, properties, reactions, and transformations, forming the basis of biochemistry, pharmacology, toxicology, and laboratory medicine.

\medterm{Chemokine receptor} A cell-surface receptor that binds chemokines and directs immune-cell movement; some also serve as entry cofactors for viruses such as HIV.

\medterm{Chemoreceptor cell} A sensory cell that detects chemical changes such as oxygen, carbon dioxide, pH, taste molecules, or odorants and converts them into neural or physiologic signals.

\medterm{Chemosensitivity assay} Chemosensitivity assay is a diagnostic test, specimen, imaging method, score, instrument, or measurement used to answer a clinical or laboratory question; results must be interpreted with the method and reference range.

\medterm{Chemosensitization} Increasing a tumor’s or pathogen’s susceptibility to a treatment by combining it with another agent that improves drug delivery, blocks resistance, or enhances cellular damage.

\medterm{Chemotactic factors} Chemical signals that attract or repel cells, especially leukocytes, by creating directional concentration gradients during inflammation, infection, development, or repair.

\medterm{Chemotherapy regimen} Chemotherapy regimen is used in relation to chemotherapy regimen during assessment, diagnosis, treatment, monitoring, or patient support.

\medterm{Chest tightness} A sensation of pressure, constriction, or heaviness in the chest that may arise from heart, lung, gastrointestinal, musculoskeletal, or anxiety-related causes.

\medterm{Chick embryo} The developing chicken embryo, widely used as an experimental model for embryology, organ development, virology, toxicology, and tissue growth.

\medterm{Child behavior} Child behavior is used in relation to child behavior during assessment, diagnosis, treatment, monitoring, or patient support.

\medterm{Child care} Child care is a medical procedure, treatment, device, or care topic; indications, benefits, risks, and follow-up depend on the patient and clinical goal.

\medterm{Child development} Progressive physical, cognitive, language, emotional, and social changes from infancy through adolescence, assessed against developmental milestones and individual variation.

\medterm{Child psychiatry} The medical specialty concerned with assessment and treatment of emotional, behavioral, developmental, and psychiatric disorders in children and adolescents.

\medterm{Child psychology} The study of children’s cognitive, emotional, social, and behavioral development and the effects of relationships, learning, biology, and environment.

\medterm{Child welfare} Services and policies intended to protect children from abuse or neglect and support safety, health, stable caregiving, development, and family wellbeing.

\medterm{Childhood angiosarcoma} Childhood angiosarcoma describes a neoplastic process involving the tissue or organ named by the term. Diagnosis establishes the tumor type, extent, biologic behavior, and appropriate management.

\medterm{Childhood ependymoma} A tumor arising from ependymal cells lining the brain ventricles or spinal canal in children; symptoms depend on location and obstruction of cerebrospinal-fluid flow.

\medterm{Childhood fibrosarcoma} Childhood fibrosarcoma describes a neoplastic process involving the tissue or organ named by the term. Diagnosis establishes the tumor type, extent, biologic behavior, and appropriate management.

\medterm{Childhood ganglioglioma} A usually slow-growing mixed neuronal and glial brain tumor in children or young adults, often presenting with long-standing focal seizures.

\medterm{Childhood leiomyosarcoma} Childhood leiomyosarcoma describes a neoplastic process involving the tissue or organ named by the term. Diagnosis establishes the tumor type, extent, biologic behavior, and appropriate management.

\medterm{Childhood leukemia} Childhood leukemia describes a neoplastic process involving the tissue or organ named by the term. Diagnosis establishes the tumor type, extent, biologic behavior, and appropriate management.

\medterm{Childhood liposarcoma} Childhood liposarcoma describes a neoplastic process involving the tissue or organ named by the term. Diagnosis establishes the tumor type, extent, biologic behavior, and appropriate management.

\medterm{Childhood osteosarcoma} Childhood osteosarcoma describes a neoplastic process involving the tissue or organ named by the term. Diagnosis establishes the tumor type, extent, biologic behavior, and appropriate management.

\medterm{Childhood rhabdomyosarcoma} Childhood rhabdomyosarcoma describes a neoplastic process involving the tissue or organ named by the term. Diagnosis establishes the tumor type, extent, biologic behavior, and appropriate management.

\medterm{Children} Humans in the developmental period between infancy and adolescence; medical assessment must account for age-specific anatomy, physiology, growth, communication, and consent.

\medterm{Chilomastix mesnili} A nonpathogenic intestinal flagellate protozoan that may be found in stool and indicates fecal exposure but usually does not require treatment.

\medterm{Chimera} An organism or tissue containing genetically distinct cell populations derived from different zygotes or embryos, or a laboratory construct combining genetic material from different sources.

\medterm{Chin} The lower facial prominence formed mainly by the mandible and overlying soft tissue, contributing to facial contour, speech, chewing, and airway anatomy.

\medterm{Chironomidae} The nonbiting midge family of insects; their larvae and body parts can act as environmental allergens and rarely trigger respiratory or skin reactions.

\medterm{Chiropractic} A healthcare practice centered mainly on manual treatment of musculoskeletal disorders, especially spinal manipulation; benefits and risks vary by condition and technique.

\medterm{Chitin} Chitin is a biochemical, genetic, or chemical term used in medicine; its significance depends on its structure, function, concentration, pathway, or exposure.

\medterm{Chitosan} A biodegradable polysaccharide derived from chitin, studied and used in wound dressings, drug delivery, hemostatic materials, and some nutritional products.

\medterm{Chlamydiaceae} Chlamydiaceae identifies chlamydiaceae, a biological taxon or infectious-disease concept whose clinical importance depends on the organism, host, and site involved.

\medterm{Chlamydomona} Chlamydomona is a biological organism or infectious agent; its clinical importance depends on the species, host, site, exposure, and evidence of infection or colonization.

\medterm{Chlamydophila} Chlamydophila is a biological organism or infectious agent; its clinical importance depends on the species, host, site, exposure, and evidence of infection or colonization.

\medterm{Chlamydophila pneumoniae} Chlamydophila pneumoniae is a biological organism or infectious agent; its clinical importance depends on the species, host, site, exposure, and evidence of infection or colonization.

\medterm{Chlamydophila psittaci} Chlamydophila psittaci is a biological organism or infectious agent; its clinical importance depends on the species, host, site, exposure, and evidence of infection or colonization.

\medterm{Chloral hydrate} Chloral hydrate is a medicine or pharmacologic agent; its indication, dosing, contraindications, interactions, and adverse effects depend on the specific product and patient.

\medterm{Chlorambucil} Chlorambucil is a medicine or pharmacologic agent; its indication, dosing, contraindications, interactions, and adverse effects depend on the specific product and patient.

\medterm{Chlordane} Chlordane is a medicine or pharmacologic agent; its indication, dosing, contraindications, interactions, and adverse effects depend on the specific product and patient.

\medterm{Crying in childhood} Vocal expression of distress or emotion in a child; persistent, inconsolable, unusual, or pain-associated crying may indicate illness, injury, abuse, or unmet developmental needs.

\medterm{Crying in infancy} Crying is an infant’s normal communication of hunger, discomfort, fatigue, or need for contact, but sudden inconsolable crying with lethargy, fever, vomiting, or injury needs urgent assessment.

\medterm{Cryotherapy for prostate cancer} Cryotherapy for prostate cancer is a procedure for cryotherapy for prostate cancer; indication, preparation, risks, technical details, and recovery depend on the patient and treatment goal.

\medterm{Cryotherapy for the skin} Cryotherapy for the skin is a procedure for cryotherapy for the skin; indication, preparation, risks, technical details, and recovery depend on the patient and treatment goal.

\medterm{Cryptosporidium enteritis} Cryptosporidium enteritis is a disorder affecting the body system or process named by the term. Clinical features and causes vary, so diagnosis is based on the history, examination, and appropriate investigations.

\medterm{CSF cell count} CSF cell count examines csf cell count; interpretation depends on the specimen or body region, test method, reference range, and clinical question.

\medterm{CSF coccidioides complement fixation test} CSF coccidioides complement fixation test examines csf coccidioides complement fixation test; interpretation depends on the specimen or body region, test method, reference range, and clinical question.

\medterm{CSF glucose test} CSF glucose test examines csf glucose test; interpretation depends on the specimen or body region, test method, reference range, and clinical question.

\medterm{CSF leak} Escape of cerebrospinal fluid through a defect in the skull base or spinal dura, causing positional headache, clear nasal or ear drainage, wound leakage, meningitis risk, or intracranial hypotension.

\medterm{CSF myelin basic protein} CSF myelin basic protein is a protein or protein-related molecule that performs a defined structural, signaling, transport, immune, or enzymatic function in cells; abnormal amounts or activity may contribute to disease.

\medterm{CSF oligoclonal banding} An electrophoretic test for restricted immunoglobulin bands in cerebrospinal fluid, interpreted with serum bands to detect intrathecal antibody production such as in multiple sclerosis.

\medterm{CSF smear} Microscopic examination of cerebrospinal fluid for cells, organisms, or abnormal material, supporting evaluation of meningitis, hemorrhage, malignancy, or inflammation.

\medterm{CSF total protein} CSF total protein is a protein or protein-related molecule that performs a defined structural, signaling, transport, immune, or enzymatic function in cells; abnormal amounts or activity may contribute to disease.

\medterm{CSF-VDRL test} CSF-VDRL test examines csf-vdrl test; interpretation depends on the specimen or body region, test method, reference range, and clinical question.

\medterm{CT angiography – chest} CT angiography – chest examines ct angiography – chest; interpretation depends on the specimen or body region, test method, reference range, and clinical question.

\medterm{CT angiography - abdomen and pelvis} CT angiography - abdomen and pelvis examines ct angiography - abdomen and pelvis; interpretation depends on the specimen or body region, test method, reference range, and clinical question.

\medterm{CT angiography - arms and legs} CT angiography - arms and legs examines ct angiography - arms and legs; interpretation depends on the specimen or body region, test method, reference range, and clinical question.

\medterm{CT angiography - head and neck} CT angiography - head and neck examines ct angiography - head and neck; interpretation depends on the specimen or body region, test method, reference range, and clinical question.

\medterm{Culture - colonic tissue} Culture - colonic tissue examines culture - colonic tissue; interpretation depends on the specimen or body region, test method, reference range, and clinical question.

\medterm{Culture - duodenal tissue} Culture - duodenal tissue examines culture - duodenal tissue; interpretation depends on the specimen or body region, test method, reference range, and clinical question.

\medterm{Culture-negative endocarditis} Culture-negative endocarditis examines culture-negative endocarditis; interpretation depends on the specimen or body region, test method, reference range, and clinical question.

\medterm{Curvature of the penis} A bend of the erect or flaccid penis caused by congenital tissue asymmetry, scar tissue such as Peyronie disease, trauma, or other conditions; it may impair intercourse or cause pain.

\medterm{Cushing syndrome due to adrenal tumor} Cushing syndrome due to adrenal tumor is a disorder affecting the body system or process named by the term. Clinical features and causes vary, so diagnosis is based on the history, examination, and appropriate investigations.

\medterm{Cutaneous skin tag} Cutaneous skin tag is a cell, tissue, or body structure with a characteristic location and function; disease involving it produces findings related to that role.

\medterm{Cuticle remover poisoning} Cuticle remover poisoning involves toxic exposure to cuticle remover poisoning; dose, route, timing, symptoms, and antidote availability determine the urgency and treatment approach.

\medterm{Cuts and puncture wounds} Cuts and puncture wounds examines cuts and puncture wounds; interpretation depends on the specimen or body region, test method, reference range, and clinical question.

\medterm{Cyanoacrylates poisoning} Cyanoacrylates poisoning involves toxic exposure to cyanoacrylates poisoning; dose, route, timing, symptoms, and antidote availability determine the urgency and treatment approach.

\medterm{Cyanotic heart disease} Cyanotic heart disease is a disorder affecting the body system or process named by the term. Clinical features and causes vary, so diagnosis is based on the history, examination, and appropriate investigations.

\medterm{Cyproheptadine overdose} Cyproheptadine overdose involves toxic exposure to cyproheptadine overdose; dose, route, timing, symptoms, and antidote availability determine the urgency and treatment approach.

\medterm{Cystic fibrosis - nutrition} Cystic fibrosis - nutrition addresses care related to cystic fibrosis - nutrition, including practical measures, warning signs, follow-up, and decisions to discuss with a clinician.

\medterm{Cystic fibrosis - resources} Cystic fibrosis - resources addresses care related to cystic fibrosis - resources, including practical measures, warning signs, follow-up, and decisions to discuss with a clinician.

\medterm{Cystitis - acute} Cystitis - acute is a disorder affecting the body system or process named by the term. Clinical features and causes vary, so diagnosis is based on the history, examination, and appropriate investigations.

\medterm{Cystitis - noninfectious} Cystitis - noninfectious examines cystitis - noninfectious; interpretation depends on the specimen or body region, test method, reference range, and clinical question.

\medterm{Cystometric study} Cystometric study is a disorder affecting the body system or process named by the term. Clinical features and causes vary, so diagnosis is based on the history, examination, and appropriate investigations.

\medterm{Cytochrome b5 reductase} Cytochrome b5 reductase examines cytochrome b5 reductase; interpretation depends on the specimen or body region, test method, reference range, and clinical question.

\medterm{Cytologic evaluation} Microscopic examination of individual cells or small cell groups from a fluid, brushing, scraping, or fine-needle sample to detect infection, inflammation, or malignancy.

\medterm{Cytology exam of pleural fluid} Microscopic examination of fluid around the lung for malignant cells and other cellular changes, performed with chemical and microbiologic testing when evaluating an effusion.

\medterm{Cytology exam of urine} Microscopic examination of shed urinary-tract cells, used mainly to detect high-grade urothelial malignancy and selected abnormal cells; a negative result does not exclude cancer.

\medterm{Calcific Bursitis} Calcific bursitis occurs when calcium deposits form in or around a bursa, often near the shoulder, causing pain and restricted movement. Treatment may include activity modification, anti-inflammatory care, physical therapy, aspiration, injection, or removal of deposits.

\medterm{Calcium Elevated (Hypercalcemia)} Alternate index label for Hypercalcemia.

\medterm{Canalith Repositioning Maneuvers} Canalith repositioning maneuvers move displaced inner-ear crystals from a semicircular canal back to the utricle. They are used for benign paroxysmal positional vertigo, with the maneuver selected according to the affected canal and side.

\medterm{Cancer Causes} Cancer develops when genetic and epigenetic changes allow cells to grow, survive, and invade abnormally. Causes and risks include inherited susceptibility, tobacco, alcohol, infections, radiation, ultraviolet exposure, carcinogens, chronic inflammation, and chance changes during cell division.

\medterm{Cancer Contagious (Is Cancer Contagious)} Alternate index label for Is Cancer Contagious.

\medterm{Cancer in lung (Lung Cancer)} Alternate index label for Lung Cancer.

\medterm{Cancer Inflammatory Breast (Breast Cancer (Facts, Stages))} Alternate index label for Breast Cancer (Facts, Stages).

\medterm{Cancer Risk (Cancer Causes)} Alternate index label for Cancer Causes.

\medterm{Candida Infection Children (Thrush and Other Yeast Infections in Children)} Alternate index label for Thrush and Other Yeast Infections in Children.

\medterm{Candidal vulvovaginitis (Yeast Infection in Women and Men)} Alternate index label for Yeast Infection in Women and Men.

\medterm{Canker Sores} Canker sores are painful, shallow ulcers on the moist lining of the mouth and are not contagious. Minor trauma, stress, nutritional deficiency, immune disease, medicines, or other triggers may contribute; persistent, severe, or recurrent ulcers need evaluation.

\medterm{Carbapenem Resistant Enterobacteriaceae (CRE Infection)} Alternate index label for CRE Infection.

\medterm{Cardiomyopathy (Restrictive)} Alternate index label for Restrictive.

\medterm{cardiospasm (Achalasia)} Alternate index label for Achalasia.

\medterm{Causes Symptoms of Hormonal Imbalances Women} Hormonal imbalance in women can reflect thyroid disease, polycystic ovary syndrome, menopause, pregnancy, adrenal or pituitary disorders, medicines, or changes in body weight. Symptoms may include irregular bleeding, acne, hair changes, infertility, hot flashes, fatigue, or mood changes.

\medterm{Cavities} A cavity is tooth decay caused by acids produced when oral bacteria metabolize dietary carbohydrates. The process can progress from enamel demineralization to dentin and pulp infection; fluoride, oral hygiene, diet, dental sealants, fillings, or root-canal treatment may be needed.

\medterm{Celiac Disease (Gluten Enteropathy)} Alternate index label for Gluten Enteropathy.

\medterm{Cells Stem (Stem Cells)} Alternate index label for Stem Cells.

\medterm{Central Precocious Puberty} Central precocious puberty begins when the hypothalamus and pituitary activate the normal puberty pathway unusually early. It causes early secondary sexual characteristics and accelerated bone maturation; evaluation identifies causes and treatment may delay progression when appropriate.

\medterm{cerebrovascular accident (Stroke Symptoms and Treatment)} Alternate index label for Stroke Symptoms and Treatment.

\medterm{Cervical Intraepithelial Neoplasia Cervical (Cervical Dysplasia)} Alternate index label for Cervical Dysplasia.

\medterm{Cervical Pain (Neck Pain)} Alternate index label for Neck Pain.

\medterm{CFS (Chronic Fatigue Syndrome)} Alternate index label for Chronic Fatigue Syndrome.

\medterm{chemotherapy treatment} Chemotherapy uses medicines that damage or inhibit rapidly dividing cancer cells. It may be given before or after local treatment, for advanced disease, or for symptom control; effects and monitoring depend on the drugs, cancer, treatment goal, and organ function.

\medterm{Chest Pain on the Left Side Above a Female Breast} Left-sided chest or breast-area pain can arise from muscle or rib injury, breast tissue, reflux, lung disease, anxiety, or heart disease. Pressure, exertional pain, shortness of breath, sweating, faintness, or pain spreading to the arm or jaw requires emergency assessment.

\medterm{CHF (Congestive Heart Failure (CHF) Overview)} Alternate index label for Congestive Heart Failure (CHF) Overview.

\medterm{Chickenpox (Varicella)} Alternate index label for Varicella.

\medterm{Chickenpox Contagious (Is Chickenpox Contagious)} Alternate index label for Is Chickenpox Contagious.

\medterm{Chiggers (Bites)} Alternate index label for Bites.

\medterm{Chikungunya Virus Infection} Chikungunya is a mosquito-borne viral infection that usually causes abrupt fever and severe joint pain, sometimes with rash, headache, or conjunctivitis. Joint symptoms can persist; care is supportive, mosquito exposure should be reduced, and severe illness requires assessment.

\medterm{Child Abuse Facts} Child abuse includes physical or emotional harm, sexual abuse, exploitation, or neglect by a caregiver or other person. Possible signs include unexplained injuries, developmental change, fear, poor hygiene, or inconsistent explanations; suspected abuse should be reported through local protection services or emergency channels.

\medterm{Children Autism (Autism and Communication)} Alternate index label for Autism and Communication.

\medterm{Children Dementia (Dementia)} Alternate index label for Dementia.

\medterm{Children Night Sweats (Night Sweats)} Alternate index label for Night Sweats.

\medterm{Children Seizures (Febrile Seizures)} Alternate index label for Febrile Seizures.

\medterm{Children Separation Anxiety (Separation Anxiety)} Alternate index label for Separation Anxiety.

\medterm{Children Sleep (Sleep and Sleep Disorders in Children and Teens)} Alternate index label for Sleep and Sleep Disorders in Children and Teens.

\medterm{Children's Cough Causes and Treatments} Children cough with viral infection, asthma, allergies, reflux, airway irritation, or an inhaled object. Treatment depends on cause and age; breathing difficulty, stridor, blue color, dehydration, sudden onset, or a cough lasting beyond expected recovery needs medical evaluation.

\medterm{Children's Health} Children's health includes growth, development, nutrition, immunization, sleep, mental health, injury prevention, and management of acute or chronic illness. Care is guided by developmental stage, family context, preventive visits, and early recognition of concerning changes.

\medterm{Childrens Health (Children's Health)} Alternate index label for Children's Health.

\medterm{chlamydia bacteria infection (Chlamydia in Women Overview)} Alternate index label for Chlamydia in Women Overview.

\medterm{Chlamydia in Women Overview} Chlamydia is a sexually transmitted infection caused by Chlamydia trachomatis and may cause no symptoms. Untreated infection can lead to cervicitis, pelvic inflammatory disease, infertility, ectopic pregnancy, or infection in a newborn; testing and treatment of partners are important.

\medterm{chloasma (Melasma)} Alternate index label for Melasma.

\medterm{Cholangiocarcinoma (Bile Duct Cancer (Cholangiocarcinoma))} Alternate index label for Bile Duct Cancer (Cholangiocarcinoma).

\medterm{Choledochal Cysts} Choledochal cysts are congenital or acquired dilations of the bile ducts that can cause abdominal pain, jaundice, pancreatitis, cholangitis, or stones. Imaging establishes the anatomy, and surgical removal is often recommended because of recurrent complications and cancer risk.

\medterm{cholelithiasis (Gallstones)} Alternate index label for Gallstones.

\medterm{Cholera Contagious (Is Cholera Contagious)} Alternate index label for Is Cholera Contagious.

\medterm{Cholesterol lowering (Cholesterol Management)} Alternate index label for Cholesterol Management.

\medterm{Cholesterol Management} Cholesterol management combines cardiovascular-risk assessment with dietary pattern, physical activity, tobacco avoidance, weight care, and medicines when indicated. Statins and other agents lower atherogenic lipoproteins, with intensity chosen according to prior cardiovascular disease and overall risk.

\medterm{chorioretinitis (Uveitis)} Alternate index label for Uveitis.

\medterm{Chorioretinitis Toxoplasma (Toxoplasmosis)} Alternate index label for Toxoplasmosis.

\medterm{Chronic Inflammatory Response Syndrome (CIRS)} Alternate index label for CIRS.

\medterm{Churg Strauss Syndrome (Churg-Strauss Syndrome)} Alternate index label for Churg-Strauss Syndrome.

\medterm{Ciguatera Poisoning} Ciguatera poisoning follows eating reef fish containing ciguatoxins. Gastrointestinal symptoms may be followed by neurologic sensations such as temperature reversal, tingling, itching, weakness, or abnormal heart rate; treatment is supportive and prevention depends on avoiding high-risk fish.

\medterm{Cirrhosis Primary Biliary (Primary Biliary Cirrhosis (PBC))} Alternate index label for Primary Biliary Cirrhosis (PBC).

\medterm{Cleft Palate and Cleft Lip} Cleft lip and cleft palate are congenital openings caused by incomplete fusion of facial structures during development. They can affect feeding, speech, hearing, teeth, and breathing and are managed by coordinated surgical, dental, speech, hearing, and nutritional care.

\medterm{Clinical depression (Depression)} Alternate index label for Depression.

\medterm{Clostridium Difficile Colitis} Clostridioides difficile colitis is antibiotic-associated inflammation of the colon caused by toxin-producing bacteria. It causes watery diarrhea, abdominal pain, fever, and sometimes severe colitis; diagnosis and treatment depend on symptoms, testing, recurrence, and severity.

\medterm{Clot Blood (Blood Clots)} Alternate index label for Blood Clots.

\medterm{Club Drugs List and Side Effects} Club drugs include substances such as MDMA, ketamine, GHB, and others used recreationally in social settings. Effects may include agitation, hallucinations, seizures, dangerous temperature elevation, respiratory depression, cardiovascular toxicity, impaired consent, and overdose.

\medterm{Cluster Headaches} Cluster headache causes repeated attacks of severe one-sided orbital or temporal pain with tearing, nasal symptoms, eyelid drooping, or restlessness. Attacks occur in bouts and require targeted acute and preventive treatment; a first sudden severe headache needs urgent evaluation.

\medterm{CMV (Cytomegalovirus (CMV))} Alternate index label for Cytomegalovirus (CMV).

\medterm{Coats Disease (Coats' Disease)} Alternate index label for Coats' Disease.

\medterm{Cocaine and Crack Abuse} Cocaine and crack use can cause dependence, agitation, paranoia, seizures, stroke, heart attack, dangerous arrhythmias, pregnancy complications, and nasal or lung injury. Treatment combines safety assessment, management of intoxication or withdrawal, behavioral care, and support for substance-use disorder.

\medterm{Cold (Common Cold)} Alternate index label for Common Cold.

\medterm{Cold and Cough Medicine for Infants and Children} Many nonprescription cold and cough medicines are unsafe or ineffective for young children and can cause sedation, agitation, abnormal heart rate, overdose, or breathing problems. Age-appropriate fluids, saline, humidification, and clinician-guided treatment are safer approaches.

\medterm{Cold Flu Allergy Treatments (Cold, Flu, Allergy)} Alternate index label for Cold, Flu, Allergy.

\medterm{Cold Sore vs. Pimple (Pimple vs Cold Sore)} Alternate index label for Pimple vs Cold Sore.

\medterm{Cold, Flu, Allergy} Colds, influenza, and allergies can all cause nasal symptoms and cough but differ in cause, fever pattern, duration, and contagiousness. Viral illness usually improves with supportive care, while allergies respond to trigger reduction and appropriate antihistamine or nasal treatment.

\medterm{Colitis Collagenous (Lymphocytic Colitis)} Alternate index label for Lymphocytic Colitis.

\medterm{Colitis Contagious (Is Colitis Contagious)} Alternate index label for Is Colitis Contagious.

\medterm{Colitis Lymphocytic (Lymphocytic Colitis)} Alternate index label for Lymphocytic Colitis.

\medterm{Colitis Microscopic (Lymphocytic Colitis)} Alternate index label for Lymphocytic Colitis.

\medterm{Colitis Ulcerative (Ulcerative Colitis)} Alternate index label for Ulcerative Colitis.

\medterm{Colon Cancer Prevention} Colorectal-cancer risk can be reduced through recommended screening, physical activity, healthy weight, limiting alcohol, avoiding tobacco, and a diet rich in fiber-containing plant foods. Screening can detect precancerous polyps and early cancer before symptoms develop.

\medterm{colonic diverticulitis (Diverticulosis)} Alternate index label for Diverticulosis.

\medterm{colorectal cancer (Colon Cancer)} Alternate index label for Colon Cancer.

\medterm{Coma Diabetic (Diabetic Ketoacidosis)} Alternate index label for Diabetic Ketoacidosis.

\medterm{Coma Myxedema (Myxedema Coma)} Alternate index label for Myxedema Coma.

\medterm{Common Cold Stages and Timeline of Symptoms} The common cold often begins with sore throat, sneezing, or fatigue, followed by nasal congestion, runny nose, and cough. Symptoms usually peak within several days and improve over one to two weeks, although cough and fatigue may last longer.

\medterm{Common Early Symptoms of Myasthenia Gravis} Early myasthenia gravis commonly causes fluctuating, fatigable weakness of the eyelids, eye movements, facial muscles, chewing, swallowing, speech, neck, or limbs. Symptoms worsen with use and improve with rest; breathing or swallowing difficulty is an emergency.

\medterm{Common Medical Abbreviations and Terms} Medical abbreviations and terms summarize diagnoses, tests, medicines, anatomy, and treatment instructions. Because abbreviations can be ambiguous or unsafe, patients should ask clinicians or pharmacists to explain unfamiliar wording rather than infer its meaning.

\medterm{Compulsive Obsessive Disorder (Obsessive Compulsive Disorder (OCD))} Alternate index label for Obsessive Compulsive Disorder (OCD).

\medterm{Conditions Caused by Deletion Mutations} Deletion mutations remove DNA segments and can disrupt one gene or several neighboring genes. The clinical effect depends on size, location, inheritance, and whether the remaining copy compensates; genetic counseling and testing may clarify diagnosis and recurrence risk.

\medterm{congenital megacolon (Hirschsprung Disease)} Alternate index label for Hirschsprung Disease.

\medterm{Congestive Heart Failure (CHF) Overview} Alternate index label for CHF) Overvie.

\medterm{Conjunctivitis (Pink Eye)} Alternate index label for Pink Eye.

\medterm{Constipation (Laxatives For Constipation)} Alternate index label for Laxatives For Constipation.

\medterm{Constipation Foods to Avoid (Top Foods That Cause Constipation)} Alternate index label for Top Foods That Cause Constipation.

\medterm{Constipation Home Remedies (Constipation)} Alternate index label for Constipation.

\medterm{consumption (Tuberculosis (TB) Facts)} Alternate index label for Tuberculosis (TB) Facts.

\medterm{Contagious C. Diff Clostridium Difficile (Is C diff Clostridium difficile Contagious)} Alternate index label for Is C diff Clostridium difficile Contagious.

\medterm{Contagious Canker Sores (Are Canker Sores Contagious)} Alternate index label for Are Canker Sores Contagious.

\medterm{Contagious Cough (Is a Cough Contagious)} Alternate index label for Is a Cough Contagious.

\medterm{Contagious Diverticulitis (Is Diverticulitis Contagious)} Alternate index label for Is Diverticulitis Contagious.

\medterm{Contagious E. coli (Is E coli Contagious)} Alternate index label for Is E coli Contagious.

\medterm{Contagious H. pylori (Is H pylori contagious)} Alternate index label for Is H pylori contagious.

\medterm{Contagious Hepatitis (Is Hepatitis Contagious)} Alternate index label for Is Hepatitis Contagious.

\medterm{Contagious Hepatitis A (Is Hepatitis A Contagious)} Alternate index label for Is Hepatitis A Contagious.

\medterm{Contagious Hepatitis B (Is Hepatitis B Contagious)} Alternate index label for Is Hepatitis B Contagious.

\medterm{Contagious Hepatitis C (Is Hepatitis C Contagious)} Alternate index label for Is Hepatitis C Contagious.

\medterm{Contagious Sinus Infection (Is a Sinus Infection Contagious)} Alternate index label for Is a Sinus Infection Contagious.

\medterm{Contagious Sore Throat (Is Sore Throat Pharyngitis Contagious)} Alternate index label for Is Sore Throat Pharyngitis Contagious.

\medterm{Contagious Strep Throat (Is Strep Throat Contagious)} Alternate index label for Is Strep Throat Contagious.

\medterm{Contagious Tonsillitis (Is Tonsillitis Contagious)} Alternate index label for Is Tonsillitis Contagious.

\medterm{Contagious Yeast (Is a Yeast Infection Contagious)} Alternate index label for Is a Yeast Infection Contagious.

\medterm{Contagious Yeast Infection (Is a Yeast Infection Contagious)} Alternate index label for Is a Yeast Infection Contagious.

\medterm{Contractions Braxton-Hicks (Braxton Hicks Contractions)} Alternate index label for Braxton Hicks Contractions.

\medterm{COPD (Chronic Obstructive Pulmonary Disease)} Alternate index label for Chronic Obstructive Pulmonary Disease.

\medterm{COPD (COPD (Chronic Obstructive Pulmonary Disease))} Alternate index label for COPD (Chronic Obstructive Pulmonary Disease).

\medterm{COPD vs. Asthma Differences and Similarities} Asthma usually has variable, reversible airway narrowing, while chronic obstructive pulmonary disease causes persistent airflow limitation associated most often with smoking or long-term irritant exposure. Both can cause cough and wheeze, but testing, course, and treatment differ.

\medterm{COPD vs. Emphysema Differences Similarities} Emphysema is structural destruction of alveoli and one component of chronic obstructive pulmonary disease. COPD may also include chronic bronchitis and small-airway disease; symptoms, spirometry, imaging, exposure history, and exacerbations guide distinction and care.

\medterm{Corneal Disease} Corneal disease affects the transparent front surface of the eye through infection, inflammation, trauma, dystrophy, degeneration, dryness, or abnormal shape. Pain, redness, light sensitivity, discharge, or reduced vision can signal urgent disease because scarring may impair sight.

\medterm{corneal inflammation (Keratitis)} Alternate index label for Keratitis.

\medterm{Corns} A corn is a localized thickening of skin caused by repeated pressure or friction, commonly on toes or other weight-bearing areas. Proper footwear, pressure relief, and treatment of the underlying deformity help; diabetes or poor circulation makes self-removal risky.

\medterm{Correct Code for Diagnostic Dilation and Curettage} Dilation and curettage is a procedure that opens the cervix and removes tissue from the uterus for diagnosis or treatment. Coding depends on the indication, setting, technique, pregnancy status, and tissue findings and should be assigned from the documented procedure.

\medterm{Cosmetic Allergies} Cosmetic products can cause irritant dermatitis or allergic contact dermatitis, producing itching, redness, swelling, scaling, or blistering where applied. Stopping the suspected product and medical assessment, sometimes with patch testing, help identify the responsible ingredient.

\medterm{Costochondritis (Costochondritis and Tietze Syndrome)} Alternate index label for Costochondritis and Tietze Syndrome.

\medterm{Costochondritis and Tietze Syndrome} Costochondritis is inflammation or irritation at the junction of ribs and the breastbone, causing reproducible chest-wall pain. Tietze syndrome is a less common related condition with visible swelling at one or a few joints; heart and lung emergencies must still be excluded.

\medterm{Cough Chronic (Chronic Cough)} Alternate index label for Chronic Cough.

\medterm{Could a Stiff Neck Be a Sign of Something Serious} Neck stiffness commonly follows muscle strain, but stiffness with fever, severe headache, light sensitivity, confusion, rash, weakness, trauma, or neurologic change can signal meningitis, bleeding, spinal injury, or another emergency.

\medterm{COVID-19 Prevention Tips} COVID-19 prevention combines staying current with recommended vaccination, improving indoor ventilation, avoiding close exposure when ill, testing when indicated, masking in higher-risk settings, and hand or respiratory hygiene. Recommendations change with local transmission and individual risk.

\medterm{COVID-19 vs. Allergies} COVID-19 may cause fever, sore throat, cough, fatigue, body aches, or loss of smell, while allergies more often cause itching, sneezing, watery eyes, and recurrent symptoms without fever. Testing and exposure history help distinguish them.

\medterm{COVID-19 vs. Flu vs. Cold} COVID-19, influenza, and common colds overlap in respiratory symptoms and cannot always be distinguished by symptoms alone. Testing, exposure, season, severity, and risk factors guide diagnosis; breathing difficulty, chest pain, confusion, or dehydration requires urgent care.

\medterm{Coxsackie (Coxsackie Virus)} Alternate index label for Coxsackie Virus.

\medterm{CPT Code for Foreign Body Removal From the Ear} Removal of an ear foreign body is coded according to the documented method, location, anesthesia, complications, and whether a separate evaluation was performed. Objects causing pain, bleeding, hearing loss, or suspected eardrum injury should be removed by a trained clinician.

\medterm{cramps but no period} Pelvic cramps without menstruation can result from ovulation, pregnancy, implantation, constipation, urinary disease, infection, endometriosis, cysts, or other pelvic conditions. Severe one-sided pain, faintness, fever, vomiting, or possible pregnancy requires urgent assessment.

\medterm{Cramps Heat (Heat Cramps)} Alternate index label for Heat Cramps.

\medterm{Cramps Menstrual (Menstrual Cramps)} Alternate index label for Menstrual Cramps.

\medterm{CRE Infection} Carbapenem-resistant Enterobacterales are bacteria resistant to important antibiotics and can cause urinary, bloodstream, abdominal, wound, or lung infections. Infection-control precautions, culture-based treatment, and specialist advice are important because therapeutic options may be limited.

\medterm{Critical Limb Ischemia vs. Acute Limb Ischemia} Chronic limb-threatening ischemia develops gradually from severe arterial disease and causes rest pain, nonhealing wounds, or tissue loss. Acute limb ischemia occurs suddenly with pain, pallor, pulselessness, numbness, weakness, or coldness and requires emergency revascularization assessment.

\medterm{Crohn Disease (Crohn's Disease)} Alternate index label for Crohn's Disease.

\medterm{Crohns Disease vs Ulcerative Colitis UC} Crohn disease can affect any part of the gastrointestinal tract and often involves patchy, full-thickness inflammation, while ulcerative colitis affects the colon continuously from the rectum with superficial mucosal inflammation. Symptoms overlap, but imaging, endoscopy, and biopsy help distinguish them.

\medterm{Crohn’s Disease Cause Dark Circles Around Eyes} Crohn disease does not directly cause dark circles in every patient, but anemia, fatigue, dehydration, poor sleep, nutritional deficiency, steroid effects, or atopic skin may contribute. Persistent pallor, fatigue, weight loss, bleeding, or active bowel symptoms deserve clinical review.

\medterm{Croup Contagious (Is Croup Contagious)} Alternate index label for Is Croup Contagious.

\medterm{Ctenocephalides felFlea Bite (Flea Bites in Humans)} Alternate index label for Flea Bites in Humans.

\medterm{Cuts wounds (Cuts, Scrapes and Puncture Wounds)} Alternate index label for Cuts, Scrapes and Puncture Wounds.

\medterm{Cuts, Scrapes and Puncture Wounds} Cuts, scrapes, and puncture wounds damage the skin to varying depths and can introduce bacteria or foreign material. Clean minor wounds with running water, control bleeding, cover them, and seek care for deep or contaminated wounds, bites, impaired circulation, infection, or uncertain tetanus protection.

\medterm{CVA (Stroke Symptoms and Treatment)} Alternate index label for Stroke Symptoms and Treatment.

\medterm{Cyclic Vomiting Syndrome (CVS)} Alternate index label for CVS.

\medterm{Cyclospora Infection (Cyclosporiasis)} Alternate index label for Cyclosporiasis.

\medterm{Cyst Eyelid (Giardia Lamblia)} Alternate index label for Giardia Lamblia.

\medterm{Cystic Acne} Cystic acne is severe inflammatory acne with deep painful nodules or cyst-like lesions that can scar. Treatment may require prescription retinoids, antimicrobial or hormonal therapy, or isotretinoin under specialist monitoring; squeezing lesions increases scarring.

\medterm{Cystitis (Bladder Infection)} Cystitis is inflammation of the bladder, most often from a bacterial urinary-tract infection. It causes burning urination, frequency, urgency, and lower-abdominal discomfort; fever, flank pain, vomiting, pregnancy, or recurrent infection requires prompt assessment.

\medterm{Cysts Choledochal (Choledochal Cysts)} Alternate index label for Choledochal Cysts.

\medterm{Cysts Ovary (Ovarian Cysts)} Alternate index label for Ovarian Cysts.

\medterm{Cysts Pancreatic Inflammatory (Pancreatic Cysts)} Alternate index label for Pancreatic Cysts.

\medterm{Cysts True Pancreas (Pancreatic Cysts)} Alternate index label for Pancreatic Cysts.

\medterm{Cytomegalovirus (CMV)} Alternate index label for CMV.

\medterm{C-Reactive Protein Test (CRP)} A blood test measuring C-reactive protein, an acute-phase protein that rises with inflammation; it is nonspecific and must be interpreted with symptoms and other findings.

\medterm{C1-C7 (cervical vertebrae)} The seven vertebrae of the neck, numbered C1 through C7; they support the head, protect the cervical spinal cord, and allow neck movement.

\medterm{CA} A medical abbreviation that may mean cancer, carcinoma, or calcium; the intended meaning depends on the surrounding clinical context.

\medterm{CA 27-29} A blood tumor-marker test measuring a form of the MUC1 antigen, used mainly to monitor some people with breast cancer rather than to screen for cancer by itself.

\medterm{Cachectic} Affected by cachexia, a severe wasting syndrome involving involuntary weight loss, muscle loss, weakness, and often reduced appetite in chronic disease.

\medterm{Cachetic} An alternate spelling of cachectic, meaning characterized by severe disease-related wasting and loss of muscle and body mass.

\medterm{CAD} Coronary artery disease, narrowing or blockage of the heart's arteries, usually from atherosclerotic plaque, that can cause angina or myocardial infarction.

\medterm{Caduceus} A staff with two entwined snakes and wings, traditionally associated with Hermes; it is often used as a medical symbol but is distinct from the single-snake Rod of Asclepius.

\medterm{Caecal} Relating to the cecum, the pouch at the beginning of the large intestine where the small intestine joins the colon.

\medterm{Caecum} The first pouch-like part of the large intestine, located where the ileum joins the colon and attached to the appendix.

\medterm{Cal (Calorie)} A unit of energy; in nutrition, a Calorie with a capital C is a kilocalorie equal to 1,000 small calories and is used to describe food energy.

\medterm{Calcaneal spur} A bony outgrowth from the heel bone, often at the plantar fascia attachment or Achilles tendon attachment; it may be asymptomatic or associated with heel pain.

\medterm{Calcified granuloma} A healed granulomatous inflammatory lesion in which calcium has been deposited, commonly seen as a small calcified focus in the lung or another organ.

\medterm{Calcium channel blocker} A drug that inhibits calcium entry through L-type calcium channels, relaxing vascular smooth muscle and, for some agents, slowing cardiac conduction and reducing contractility.

\medterm{Calcium deficiency} Inadequate calcium intake or availability; severe deficiency can contribute to hypocalcemia, muscle spasms, impaired bone mineralization, and increased fracture risk.

\medterm{Calculus, renal} A kidney stone, a hard mass of mineral crystals formed in the urinary tract that may cause colicky pain, hematuria, obstruction, or infection.

\medterm{Calf} The posterior portion of the lower leg between the knee and ankle, containing the gastrocnemius, soleus, and other muscles.

\medterm{Calipers} An instrument with opposing arms used to measure distance, thickness, or body dimensions, including skinfold thickness in nutritional assessment.

\medterm{Calor} Heat or increased warmth of a body part, one of the classic local signs of inflammation.

\medterm{Calor, dolor, rubor, and tumor} The classic signs of inflammation: heat, pain, redness, and swelling; inflammation may also impair function.

\medterm{Cannabinoid hyperemesis syndrome (CHS)} A syndrome of recurrent severe nausea, vomiting, and abdominal pain associated with prolonged frequent cannabis use; symptoms often improve after sustained cannabis cessation.

\medterm{caps} A prescription abbreviation for capsules, oral dosage forms in which medicine is enclosed in a soluble shell.

\medterm{Carboxyhemoglobinemia} An abnormally high level of carboxyhemoglobin formed when carbon monoxide binds hemoglobin, reducing oxygen delivery and impairing oxygen release to tissues.

\medterm{Carcinogenic} Capable of causing or promoting cancer through genetic damage, altered cell regulation, or other mechanisms.

\medterm{Cardiopathy} Any disease or disorder of the heart, including diseases of the heart muscle, valves, coronary arteries, rhythm, or congenital structure.

\medterm{Carotid} Relating to either of the major arteries in the neck that carry blood from the heart toward the brain and head.

\medterm{Cascade} A sequence in which one activated reaction or event triggers the next, such as the coagulation, complement, or inflammatory cascade.

\medterm{Caseous} Having a cheese-like appearance or consistency, especially the necrotic material typical of some tuberculosis granulomas.

\medterm{Cashew allergy} An immune reaction to cashew proteins that can cause hives, swelling, vomiting, wheezing, or life-threatening anaphylaxis.

\medterm{Cassette} A holder or container for recording or imaging material; in radiography, a cassette traditionally houses the film or image receptor during exposure.

\medterm{CAT scan} Computed axial tomography, commonly called CT, an imaging method that uses multiple X-ray measurements and computer processing to create cross-sectional body images.

\medterm{Cataplasm} A poultice: a soft, moist substance spread on cloth and applied to the body, historically for soothing, warming, or treating a local condition.

\medterm{Category five hurricane} A Saffir--Simpson hurricane with sustained winds of at least 157 miles per hour (252 km/h), capable of catastrophic damage and prolonged loss of habitability.
